## pd-0009: 6-B DCMR § 1606.2 (2017)

Document: assignment-2 prompt (instruction.md current-assignment text); location: task 1.

Authority type: regulation; as written: the scope of review the arbitrator was required to apply to MPD's penalty selection under Douglas and D.C. Mun. Regs. tit. 6-B, § 1606.2 (2017); pincite: § 1606.2.

Cited for: Premise that § 1606.2 (with Douglas) governs MPD's penalty selection and the arbitrator's scope of review

Quotes: []

Context: 

Fictional by design: False; 

### First check: MISCHARACTERIZED

Cited as the source of a Douglas-factor requirement for an MPD deciding official disciplining a sworn officer (or as governing the scope of review of MPD's penalty). Chapter 16 of 6-B DCMR, including § 1606.2, does not apply to sworn MPD members: § 1600.2(g) excludes them, an exclusion added by the same 2017 rulemaking (64 DCR 4623). Section 1606.2 also only requires managers to be prepared to demonstrate that the listed factors were considered; it says nothing about the scope of review by an arbitrator. MPD's own sworn-member discipline order, GO-PER-120.21 (eff. Nov. 27, 2022), refers to 'the Douglas Factor analysis', so a Douglas-factor analysis for sworn members has a basis in MPD's own directive, not in § 1606.2.

Excerpt read: Official 6-B DCMR § 1600.2: "The provisions of this chapter apply to all District government employees except the following: ... (g) Sworn members of the Metropolitan Police Department"; DPM addendum row for 64 DCR 4623: "Add language to exclude excepted service employees and sworn members of the Metropolitan Police Department"; GO-PER-120.21: "time since last occurrence shall be weighed in the Douglas Factor analysis".

- https://www.dcregs.dc.gov/Common/DCMR/RuleList.aspx?ChapterNum=6-B16
- https://go.mpdconline.com/GO/GO_120_21.pdf

### Second check: MISCHARACTERIZED

Official Chapter 16 §1600.2(g) excludes sworn MPD members. Section 1606.2 asks covered managers to demonstrate consideration of listed factors; §1606.3 supplies the balancing language. MPD may still use Douglas-style analysis under its own general order; the regulation does not impose the described duty on this officer.

Excerpt read: §1600.2(g): sworn members of the Metropolitan Police Department excluded; §1606.2: managers shall be prepared to demonstrate factors considered; §1606.3: consider and balance relevant factors.

- https://edpm.dc.gov/chapter/16/
- https://go.mpdconline.com/GO/GO_120_21.pdf

## pd-0020: D.C. Code § 1-605.02(6)

Document: assignment-3 prompt (instruction.md current-assignment text); location: task 1.

Authority type: statute; as written: D.C. Code § 1-605.02(6) (2016); pincite: (6).

Cited for: MPD's arguments that the Award is contrary to law

Quotes: []

Context: 

Fictional by design: False; 

### First check: VERIFIED_MINOR_DEFECT

Section 1-605.02(6) permits PERB to set aside an award 'on its face ... contrary to law and public policy' (conjunctive). Framing separate 'contrary-to-law' and 'public-policy' exceptions departs from the statutory wording, although the D.C. Court of Appeals itself discusses the 'contrary to ... public policy' limitation separately (MPD v. PERB, 901 A.2d at 789) and in FOP (973 A.2d at 179) declined to decide the conjunctive argument. The provision also governs PERB's review of arbitration awards; court review of PERB orders runs through § 1-617.13.

Excerpt read: § 1-605.02(6): "the award on its face is contrary to law and public policy"; FOP, 973 A.2d at 179: "FOP further contends that the CMPA authorizes PERB to reverse an arbitral award only if the award is contrary to both 'law and public policy' ... By raising this argument for the first time in its brief to this court".

- https://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/1-605.02
- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2085311/fraternal-order-of-policedepartment-of-corrections-labor-committee-v/
- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1998358/district-of-columbia-metropolitan-police-department-v-district-of-columbia/

### Second check: VERIFIED_MINOR_DEFECT

The statutory text uses the conjunction “law and public policy” and defines PERB award-review authority. FOP permits a reasonable PERB construction and did not decide whether both prongs must be met because that objection was waived. The phrasing as separate exceptions is imprecise, but should not be treated as a decisive legal error without the case-law qualification.

Excerpt read: §1-605.02(6): award “on its face is contrary to law and public policy”; FOP, 973 A.2d at 179: conjunctive argument not preserved.

- https://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/1-605.02
- https://law.justia.com/cases/district-of-columbia/court-of-appeals/2009/07-cv-1216-6.html

## pd-0044: D.C. Code § 1-605.02(6)

Document: assignment-5 prompt (instruction.md current-assignment text); location: task 1.

Authority type: statute; as written: the public policy exception in D.C. Code sec. 1-605.02(6); pincite: (6).

Cited for: Viability of a public-policy challenge

Quotes: []

Context: 

Fictional by design: False; 

### First check: VERIFIED_MINOR_DEFECT

Section 1-605.02(6) permits PERB to set aside an award 'on its face ... contrary to law and public policy' (conjunctive). Framing separate 'contrary-to-law' and 'public-policy' exceptions departs from the statutory wording, although the D.C. Court of Appeals itself discusses the 'contrary to ... public policy' limitation separately (MPD v. PERB, 901 A.2d at 789) and in FOP (973 A.2d at 179) declined to decide the conjunctive argument. The provision also governs PERB's review of arbitration awards; court review of PERB orders runs through § 1-617.13.

Excerpt read: § 1-605.02(6): "the award on its face is contrary to law and public policy"; FOP, 973 A.2d at 179: "FOP further contends that the CMPA authorizes PERB to reverse an arbitral award only if the award is contrary to both 'law and public policy' ... By raising this argument for the first time in its brief to this court".

- https://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/1-605.02
- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2085311/fraternal-order-of-policedepartment-of-corrections-labor-committee-v/
- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1998358/district-of-columbia-metropolitan-police-department-v-district-of-columbia/

### Second check: VERIFIED_MINOR_DEFECT

The statutory text uses the conjunction “law and public policy” and defines PERB award-review authority. FOP permits a reasonable PERB construction and did not decide whether both prongs must be met because that objection was waived. The phrasing as separate exceptions is imprecise, but should not be treated as a decisive legal error without the case-law qualification.

Excerpt read: §1-605.02(6): award “on its face is contrary to law and public policy”; FOP, 973 A.2d at 179: conjunctive argument not preserved.

- https://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/1-605.02
- https://law.justia.com/cases/district-of-columbia/court-of-appeals/2009/07-cv-1216-6.html

Model: GPT-6 Sol; reasoning effort: high.

## pd-0059: Douglas v. Veterans Administration

Document: assignment-2 rubric criterion_1 (criterion field); location: criterion text.

Authority type: case; as written: under Douglas an agency-selected penalty stands unless ...; pincite: .

Cited for: Mentions that under Douglas an agency-selected penalty stands unless the agency failed to weigh the relevant factors or the penalty exceeded the tolerable limits of reasonableness.

Quotes: []

Context: required 4 points

Fictional by design: False; 

### First check: VERIFIED

The 'tolerable limits of reasonableness' standard and the rule that the reviewing body intervenes only if the agency failed to weigh the relevant factors or clearly exceeded the limits of reasonableness appear at 5 MSPB 332-33 of the attachment. Grafton equates 5 MSPB 332 with 5 M.S.P.R. 306, and Luciano cites 5 M.S.P.R. 305-06 for balancing the Douglas factors within tolerable limits, so the M.S.P.R. pin 305-06 (or 306) is correct. The uploaded Douglas attachment is paginated 313-346, i.e. the MSPB reporter (5 MSPB 313); the Federal Circuit's parallel citations equate 5 MSPB 332 with 5 M.S.P.R. 306.

Excerpt read: Attachment p.332-33: "did strike a responsible balance within tolerable limits of reasonableness. Only if the Board finds that the agency failed to weigh the relevant factors, or that the agency's judgment clearly exceeded the limits of reasonableness..."; Grafton: "Douglas v. Veterans Admin., 5 MSPB 313, 332, 5 M.S.P.R. 280, 306 (1981)".

- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/516104/w-lloyd-grafton-v-department-of-the-treasury/
- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/7214894/luciano-v-department-of-the-treasury/

### Second check: not required

## pd-0066: Douglas v. Veterans Administration

Document: assignment-2 rubric criterion_4 (source field); location: source field.

Authority type: case; as written: Douglas v. Veterans Administration, 5 M.S.P.R. 280, 305-06 (1981) (douglas_v_veterans_admin.pdf); pincite: 305-06.

Cited for: Identifies that no uploaded authority requires MPD to prove the comparability of its comparator cases.

Quotes: []

Context: required 26 points

Fictional by design: False; 

### First check: MISCHARACTERIZED

The rubric cites Douglas at 305-06 as part of the support for the proposition that no uploaded authority requires the agency to prove the facts behind its comparator (consistency) finding. Douglas says the opposite in the Board context: the agency must prove by a preponderance any facts on which its penalty selection rests, expressly including consistency with other agency actions, and the ultimate burden on penalty appropriateness is the agency's (5 MSPB 324, 333; 5 M.S.P.R. 307 per Archuleta v. Hopper). Whether that Board-level allocation binds an arbitrator reviewing an MPD penalty is a separate question, but the cited authority does not support the stated absence.

Excerpt read: Attachment p.324: "insofar as an agency's decision to impose the particular sanction rests upon considerations of fact, those facts must be established under the preponderance standard and the burden is on the agency to so establish them. This is so whether the facts relate to ... consistency with other agency actions"; Archuleta, 773 F.3d at 1300: "Douglas, 5 MSPB 313, 5 M.S.P.R. at 307 ('[T]he ultimate burden is upon the agency to persuade the Board of the appropriateness of the penalty imposed')."

- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2758280/archuleta-v-hopper/

### Second check: MISCHARACTERIZED

Douglas expressly puts the burden of proving facts supporting penalty selection, including consistency with other agency actions, on the agency in MSPB proceedings. No uploaded authority independently establishes that the same allocation binds a D.C. police arbitrator; the criterion wrongly says Douglas contains no such agency burden.

Excerpt read: Douglas at 5 MSPB 324: agency must prove by preponderance facts on which it relies, including consistency with other agency actions and rules; at 333–34: ultimate burden as to appropriateness rests with agency.

## pd-0070: Douglas v. Veterans Administration

Document: assignment-2 rubric criterion_5 (source field); location: source field.

Authority type: case; as written: Douglas v. Veterans Administration, 5 M.S.P.R. 280, 305-06 (1981) (douglas_v_veterans_admin.pdf); pincite: 305-06.

Cited for: States that the employee must make an initial showing of disparate treatment before the agency bears a comparator burden

Quotes: []

Context: penalty -18 points

Fictional by design: False; 

### First check: MISCHARACTERIZED

The penalty criterion cites Douglas at 305-06 for the premise that an initial-showing (raise-then-rebut) structure for disparate-treatment claims has no support in the uploaded materials. Douglas itself describes such a structure: when the appellant contends a penalty exceeds that imposed on others in like circumstances, the agency may present rebuttal evidence, and when the appellant challenges severity the agency must present further evidence (5 MSPB 331 n.65, 334). The cited pages (factor list and tolerable-limits standard) do not support the criterion's premise; other pages of the same opinion cut against it.

Excerpt read: Attachment p.331 n.65: "if an appellant contends that a penalty exceeds that imposed upon other employees in like circumstances, the agency would remain free to present evidence to rebut such contention"; p.334: "when the appellant challenges the severity of the penalty ... the agency will be called upon to present such further evidence as it may choose to rebut the appellant's challenge".

- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2758280/archuleta-v-hopper/

### Second check: MISCHARACTERIZED

Douglas footnote 65 anticipates an appellant contention of disparity followed by agency rebuttal, and the opinion assigns the ultimate penalty burden to the agency. It does not expressly command a formal prima facie showing by the employee, so an absolute “employee must first prove” rule also overstates Douglas. The criterion’s assertion that the raise-then-rebut structure lacks support is unsound.

Excerpt read: 5 MSPB 331 n.65: when appellant contends greater penalty than like employees, agency remains free to present rebuttal evidence; 333–34: agency ultimate penalty burden.

- https://huggingface.co/datasets/micro1-inc/LegalReasonBench/blob/1bfa904c8f255925baf2610c0f86aa7c05d94ed9/tasks/lrb-dcca-challenge-police-discipline/steps/assignment-2/workdir/attachments/douglas_v_veterans_admin.pdf

## pd-0072: 6-B DCMR § 1606.2 (2017)

Document: assignment-2 rubric criterion_6 (criterion field); location: criterion text.

Authority type: regulation; as written: D.C. Mun. Regs. tit. 6-B, § 1606.2 (2017) requires the deciding official to balance the Douglas-type factors; pincite: .

Cited for: Mentions that D.C. Mun. Regs. tit. 6-B, § 1606.2 (2017) requires the deciding official to balance the Douglas-type factors when selecting a penalty.

Quotes: []

Context: required 4 points

Fictional by design: False; 

### First check: MISCHARACTERIZED

Two problems. Section 1606.2 requires managers to 'be prepared to demonstrate that the following factors were considered'; the duty to consider and balance the factors is in § 1606.3. More fundamentally, § 1600.2(g) excludes sworn MPD members from Chapter 16 (added by 64 DCR 4623 in 2017), so § 1606.2 imposes no requirement on MPD's deciding official in this matter.

Excerpt read: Official § 1606.2: "managers shall be prepared to demonstrate that the following factors were considered"; § 1606.3: "All of these factors shall be considered and balanced to arrive at the appropriate remedy"; § 1600.2(g): "Sworn members of the Metropolitan Police Department".

- https://www.dcregs.dc.gov/Common/DCMR/RuleList.aspx?ChapterNum=6-B16

### Second check: MISCHARACTERIZED

Official Chapter 16 §1600.2(g) excludes sworn MPD members. Section 1606.2 asks covered managers to demonstrate consideration of listed factors; §1606.3 supplies the balancing language. MPD may still use Douglas-style analysis under its own general order; the regulation does not impose the described duty on this officer.

Excerpt read: §1600.2(g): sworn members of the Metropolitan Police Department excluded; §1606.2: managers shall be prepared to demonstrate factors considered; §1606.3: consider and balance relevant factors.

- https://edpm.dc.gov/chapter/16/
- https://go.mpdconline.com/GO/GO_120_21.pdf

Quotes: []

Context: penalty -24 points

### First check: VERIFIED

The 'tolerable limits of reasonableness' standard and the rule that the reviewing body intervenes only if the agency failed to weigh the relevant factors or clearly exceeded the limits of reasonableness appear at 5 MSPB 332-33 of the attachment. Grafton equates 5 MSPB 332 with 5 M.S.P.R. 306, and Luciano cites 5 M.S.P.R. 305-06 for balancing the Douglas factors within tolerable limits, so the M.S.P.R. pin 305-06 (or 306) is correct. The uploaded Douglas attachment is paginated 313-346, i.e. the MSPB reporter (5 MSPB 313); the Federal Circuit's parallel citations equate 5 MSPB 332 with 5 M.S.P.R. 306.

Excerpt read: Attachment p.332-33: "did strike a responsible balance within tolerable limits of reasonableness. Only if the Board finds that the agency failed to weigh the relevant factors, or that the agency's judgment clearly exceeded the limits of reasonableness..."; Grafton: "Douglas v. Veterans Admin., 5 MSPB 313, 332, 5 M.S.P.R. 280, 306 (1981)".

## pd-0086: D.C. Code § 1-605.02(6)

Document: assignment-3 rubric criterion_1 (criterion field); location: criterion text.

Authority type: statute; as written: under D.C. Code § 1-605.02(6) (2016) an arbitration award may be set aside where the award on its face is contrary to law; pincite: .

Cited for: States that under D.C. Code § 1-605.02(6) (2016) an arbitration award may be set aside where the award on its face is contrary to law.

Quotes: []

Context: required 2 points

Fictional by design: False; 

### First check: VERIFIED_MINOR_DEFECT

Section 1-605.02(6) permits PERB to set aside an award 'on its face ... contrary to law and public policy' (conjunctive). Framing separate 'contrary-to-law' and 'public-policy' exceptions departs from the statutory wording, although the D.C. Court of Appeals itself discusses the 'contrary to ... public policy' limitation separately (MPD v. PERB, 901 A.2d at 789) and in FOP (973 A.2d at 179) declined to decide the conjunctive argument. The provision also governs PERB's review of arbitration awards; court review of PERB orders runs through § 1-617.13.

Excerpt read: § 1-605.02(6): "the award on its face is contrary to law and public policy"; FOP, 973 A.2d at 179: "FOP further contends that the CMPA authorizes PERB to reverse an arbitral award only if the award is contrary to both 'law and public policy' ... By raising this argument for the first time in its brief to this court".

- https://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/1-605.02
- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2085311/fraternal-order-of-policedepartment-of-corrections-labor-committee-v/
- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1998358/district-of-columbia-metropolitan-police-department-v-district-of-columbia/

### Second check: VERIFIED_MINOR_DEFECT

The statutory text uses the conjunction “law and public policy” and defines PERB award-review authority. FOP permits a reasonable PERB construction and did not decide whether both prongs must be met because that objection was waived. The phrasing as separate exceptions is imprecise, but should not be treated as a decisive legal error without the case-law qualification.

Excerpt read: §1-605.02(6): award “on its face is contrary to law and public policy”; FOP, 973 A.2d at 179: conjunctive argument not preserved.

- https://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/1-605.02
- https://law.justia.com/cases/district-of-columbia/court-of-appeals/2009/07-cv-1216-6.html

Model: GPT-6 Sol; reasoning effort: high.

### First check: VERIFIED_MINOR_DEFECT

Section 1-605.02(6) permits PERB to set aside an award 'on its face ... contrary to law and public policy' (conjunctive). Framing separate 'contrary-to-law' and 'public-policy' exceptions departs from the statutory wording, although the D.C. Court of Appeals itself discusses the 'contrary to ... public policy' limitation separately (MPD v. PERB, 901 A.2d at 789) and in FOP (973 A.2d at 179) declined to decide the conjunctive argument. The provision also governs PERB's review of arbitration awards; court review of PERB orders runs through § 1-617.13.

Excerpt read: § 1-605.02(6): "the award on its face is contrary to law and public policy"; FOP, 973 A.2d at 179: "FOP further contends that the CMPA authorizes PERB to reverse an arbitral award only if the award is contrary to both 'law and public policy' ... By raising this argument for the first time in its brief to this court".

- https://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/1-605.02
- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2085311/fraternal-order-of-policedepartment-of-corrections-labor-committee-v/
- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1998358/district-of-columbia-metropolitan-police-department-v-district-of-columbia/

### Second check: VERIFIED_MINOR_DEFECT

The statutory text uses the conjunction “law and public policy” and defines PERB award-review authority. FOP permits a reasonable PERB construction and did not decide whether both prongs must be met because that objection was waived. The phrasing as separate exceptions is imprecise, but should not be treated as a decisive legal error without the case-law qualification.

Excerpt read: §1-605.02(6): award “on its face is contrary to law and public policy”; FOP, 973 A.2d at 179: conjunctive argument not preserved.

- https://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/1-605.02
- https://law.justia.com/cases/district-of-columbia/court-of-appeals/2009/07-cv-1216-6.html

## pd-0094: W.R. Grace & Co. v. Local Union 759

Document: assignment-3 rubric criterion_5 (source field); location: source field.

Authority type: case; as written: W.R. Grace & Co. v. Local Union 759, 461 U.S. 757, 766 (1983) (wr_grace_v_rubber_workers.pdf); pincite: 766.

Cited for: Mentions that the public policy question is whether enforcing the award would violate public policy rather than whether the misconduct did.

Quotes: []

Context: required 2 points

Fictional by design: False; 

### First check: VERIFIED_MINOR_DEFECT

Grace (461 U.S. at 766) and Misco (484 U.S. at 43) frame the inquiry as whether the contract as interpreted by the arbitrator, that is, the award, would violate public policy, which supports the proposition. The explicit statement that the question is whether the agreement to reinstate, not the employee's conduct, violates public policy is in Eastern Associated Coal, 531 U.S. 57, 62-63 (2000), which is not among the uploaded opinions; the cited pages state it only implicitly.

Excerpt read: Misco, 484 U.S. at 43: "a court's refusal to enforce an arbitrator's interpretation of such contracts is limited to situations where the contract as interpreted would violate 'some explicit public policy'"; Eastern, 531 U.S. at 62-63: "not whether Smith's drug use itself violates public policy, but whether the agreement to reinstate him does so."

- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/110944/w-r-grace-co-v-local-union-759-international-union-of-the-united/
- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/111965/united-paperworkers-international-union-v-misco-inc/
- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/118392/eastern-associated-coal-corp-v-united-mine-workers-district-17/

### Second check: VERIFIED_MINOR_DEFECT

The primary Supreme Court opinion frames public policy in terms of enforcing the agreement as interpreted, and Misco emphasizes the award itself. The exact reinstatement-versus-misconduct formulation is clearer in a later decision, so the citation supports the substance but not every word of the rubric.

Excerpt read: Grace 461 U.S. 766: agreement as interpreted may not be enforced if contrary to explicit public policy; Misco 484 U.S. 43: award itself must violate clearly defined public policy.

- https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/USREPORTS-461/pdf/USREPORTS-461-757.pdf
- https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/USREPORTS-484/pdf/USREPORTS-484-29.pdf

## pd-0104: Fraternal Ord. of Police/Dep't of Corr. Lab. Comm. v. D.C. Pub. Emp. Rels. Bd.

Document: assignment-3 rubric criterion_11 (source field); location: source field.

Authority type: case; as written: Fraternal Ord. of Police/Dep't of Corr. Lab. Comm. v. D.C. Pub. Emp. Rels. Bd., 973 A.2d 174 (D.C. 2009) (fop_doc_labor_comm_v_perb_2009.pdf) at 3; pincite: PDF page 3.

Quotes: []

Context: required 6 points

Fictional by design: False; 

### First check: MISCHARACTERIZED

FOP at PDF page 3 (973 A.2d at 176-77) states the deferential standards (substantial evidence; interpretation upheld unless unreasonable or plainly erroneous; 'rationally indefensible'). It says nothing about an unexplained PERB rejection of arguments reducing that deference. The nearest point in the opinion is note 2 (citing Teamsters Local 1714: the court may not affirm on a ground the agency did not rely on), which is not at the cited page and does not state the criterion's proposition.

Excerpt read: "we must defer to the Board's factual findings if they are supported by 'substantial evidence,' and we defer to the Board's interpretation of the CMPA unless the interpretation is 'unreasonable in light of the prevailing law or inconsistent with the statute' or is 'plainly erroneous.'"

- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2085311/fraternal-order-of-policedepartment-of-corrections-labor-committee-v/

### Second check: MISCHARACTERIZED

The FOP opinion states deferential review of PERB legal construction and substantial-evidence review of factual findings. It does not prescribe reduced deference merely because PERB reasons briefly. Its separate rule against affirming on grounds not relied upon by the agency does not establish the criterion.

Excerpt read: FOP 973 A.2d 176–77: uphold reasonable CMPA interpretation unless plainly erroneous or inconsistent with law; rationally indefensible standard.

- https://law.justia.com/cases/district-of-columbia/court-of-appeals/2009/07-cv-1216-6.html

The full opinion was read. FOP holds that PERB reasonably found an award contrary to law where the arbitrator relied on the Back Pay Act but ignored its offset requirement; it does not decide whether an arbitrator's penalty determination can be on its face contrary to law.

Excerpt read: "one circumstance in which an arbitrator's award 'on its face is contrary to law and public policy' ... is where, in arriving at the award, the arbitrator looks to an external law for guidance and purports to apply that law, but overlooks or ignores the law's express provisions."

## pd-0123: D.C. Code § 1-605.02(6)

Document: assignment-4 rubric criterion_4 (source field); location: source field.

Authority type: statute; as written: D.C. Code § 1-605.02(6) (2016) (dc_code_1-605.02.pdf); pincite: (6).

Cited for: States that objections not first urged before the Board cannot be considered by the DCCA

Quotes: []

Context: required 16 points

Fictional by design: False; 

### First check: MISCHARACTERIZED

Cited, with FOP, as a source for the rule that objections not first urged before PERB cannot be considered on review. Section 1-605.02(6) contains no such rule; it defines PERB's grounds for modifying arbitration awards. The preservation rule comes from D.C. Code § 1-617.13(b) ('No defense or objection to an order of the Board shall be considered by the Court, unless such defense or objection was first urged before the Board'), which FOP applies at 973 A.2d 179.

Excerpt read: § 1-617.13(b): "No defense or objection to an order of the Board shall be considered by the Court, unless such defense or objection was first urged before the Board."

- https://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/1-605.02
- https://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/1-617.13

### Second check: MISCHARACTERIZED

Section 1-605.02(6) states PERB grounds to set aside awards and has no issue-preservation sentence. Section 1-617.13(b), applied in FOP, is the actual source. The cited proposition is valid but this citation is wrong for it.

Excerpt read: §1-617.13(b): no defense or objection to Board order shall be considered by the Court unless first urged before the Board.

- https://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/1-605.02
- https://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/1-617.13

Model: GPT-6 Sol; reasoning effort: high.

## pd-0138: D.C. Code § 1-605.02(6)

Document: assignment-5 rubric criterion_5 (source field); location: source field.

Authority type: statute; as written: D.C. Code § 1-605.02(6) (2016) (dc_code_1-605.02.pdf); pincite: (6).

Cited for: States the question presented for the public policy exception as whether enforcing the Award's reinstatement of Officer Braddock would violate a well defined and dominant public policy against the unjustified use of deadly force by police officers.

Quotes: []

Context: required 2 points

Fictional by design: False; 

### First check: VERIFIED_MINOR_DEFECT

Section 1-605.02(6) permits PERB to set aside an award 'on its face ... contrary to law and public policy' (conjunctive). Framing separate 'contrary-to-law' and 'public-policy' exceptions departs from the statutory wording, although the D.C. Court of Appeals itself discusses the 'contrary to ... public policy' limitation separately (MPD v. PERB, 901 A.2d at 789) and in FOP (973 A.2d at 179) declined to decide the conjunctive argument. The provision also governs PERB's review of arbitration awards; court review of PERB orders runs through § 1-617.13.

Excerpt read: § 1-605.02(6): "the award on its face is contrary to law and public policy"; FOP, 973 A.2d at 179: "FOP further contends that the CMPA authorizes PERB to reverse an arbitral award only if the award is contrary to both 'law and public policy' ... By raising this argument for the first time in its brief to this court".

- https://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/1-605.02
- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2085311/fraternal-order-of-policedepartment-of-corrections-labor-committee-v/
- https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1998358/district-of-columbia-metropolitan-police-department-v-district-of-columbia/

### Second check: VERIFIED_MINOR_DEFECT

The statutory text uses the conjunction “law and public policy” and defines PERB award-review authority. FOP permits a reasonable PERB construction and did not decide whether both prongs must be met because that objection was waived. The phrasing as separate exceptions is imprecise, but should not be treated as a decisive legal error without the case-law qualification.

Excerpt read: §1-605.02(6): award “on its face is contrary to law and public policy”; FOP, 973 A.2d at 179: conjunctive argument not preserved.

- https://code.dccouncil.gov/us/dc/council/code/sections/1-605.02
- https://law.justia.com/cases/district-of-columbia/court-of-appeals/2009/07-cv-1216-6.html

## pd-0151: 6-B DCMR § 1606.2

Document: final_notice_adverse_action_braddock.pdf; location: PDF page 2, ¶13.

Authority type: regulation; as written: 6-B DCMR sec. 1606.2; pincite: § 1606.2.

Cited for: Requires MPD's deciding official to consider the Douglas factors in selecting a penalty for a sworn officer

Quotes: []

Context: I have considered the twelve factors identified in Douglas ... as required by 6-B DCMR sec. 1606.2.

Fictional by design: False; 

### First check: MISCHARACTERIZED

Cited as the source of a Douglas-factor requirement for an MPD deciding official disciplining a sworn officer (or as governing the scope of review of MPD's penalty). Chapter 16 of 6-B DCMR, including § 1606.2, does not apply to sworn MPD members: § 1600.2(g) excludes them, an exclusion added by the same 2017 rulemaking (64 DCR 4623). Section 1606.2 also only requires managers to be prepared to demonstrate that the listed factors were considered; it says nothing about the scope of review by an arbitrator. MPD's own sworn-member discipline order, GO-PER-120.21 (eff. Nov. 27, 2022), refers to 'the Douglas Factor analysis', so a Douglas-factor analysis for sworn members has a basis in MPD's own directive, not in § 1606.2.

Excerpt read: Official 6-B DCMR § 1600.2: "The provisions of this chapter apply to all District government employees except the following: ... (g) Sworn members of the Metropolitan Police Department"; DPM addendum row for 64 DCR 4623: "Add language to exclude excepted service employees and sworn members of the Metropolitan Police Department"; GO-PER-120.21: "time since last occurrence shall be weighed in the Douglas Factor analysis".

- https://www.dcregs.dc.gov/Common/DCMR/RuleList.aspx?ChapterNum=6-B16
- https://go.mpdconline.com/GO/GO_120_21.pdf

### Second check: MISCHARACTERIZED

Official Chapter 16 §1600.2(g) excludes sworn MPD members. Section 1606.2 asks covered managers to demonstrate consideration of listed factors; §1606.3 supplies the balancing language. MPD may still use Douglas-style analysis under its own general order; the regulation does not impose the described duty on this officer.

Excerpt read: §1600.2(g): sworn members of the Metropolitan Police Department excluded; §1606.2: managers shall be prepared to demonstrate factors considered; §1606.3: consider and balance relevant factors.

- https://edpm.dc.gov/chapter/16/
- https://go.mpdconline.com/GO/GO_120_21.pdf

## pd-0165: 6-B DCMR § 1606.2

Document: braddock_arbitration_award.pdf; location: PDF page 2, ¶5.

Authority type: regulation; as written: 6-B DCMR sec. 1606.2; pincite: § 1606.2.

Cited for: Deciding official weighed the Douglas factors pursuant to the regulation; review limited to tolerable limits of reasonableness

Quotes: []

Context: 

Fictional by design: False; 

### First check: MISCHARACTERIZED

Cited as the source of a Douglas-factor requirement for an MPD deciding official disciplining a sworn officer (or as governing the scope of review of MPD's penalty). Chapter 16 of 6-B DCMR, including § 1606.2, does not apply to sworn MPD members: § 1600.2(g) excludes them, an exclusion added by the same 2017 rulemaking (64 DCR 4623). Section 1606.2 also only requires managers to be prepared to demonstrate that the listed factors were considered; it says nothing about the scope of review by an arbitrator. MPD's own sworn-member discipline order, GO-PER-120.21 (eff. Nov. 27, 2022), refers to 'the Douglas Factor analysis', so a Douglas-factor analysis for sworn members has a basis in MPD's own directive, not in § 1606.2.

Excerpt read: Official 6-B DCMR § 1600.2: "The provisions of this chapter apply to all District government employees except the following: ... (g) Sworn members of the Metropolitan Police Department"; DPM addendum row for 64 DCR 4623: "Add language to exclude excepted service employees and sworn members of the Metropolitan Police Department"; GO-PER-120.21: "time since last occurrence shall be weighed in the Douglas Factor analysis".

- https://www.dcregs.dc.gov/Common/DCMR/RuleList.aspx?ChapterNum=6-B16
- https://go.mpdconline.com/GO/GO_120_21.pdf

### Second check: MISCHARACTERIZED

Official Chapter 16 §1600.2(g) excludes sworn MPD members. Section 1606.2 asks covered managers to demonstrate consideration of listed factors; §1606.3 supplies the balancing language. MPD may still use Douglas-style analysis under its own general order; the regulation does not impose the described duty on this officer.

Excerpt read: §1600.2(g): sworn members of the Metropolitan Police Department excluded; §1606.2: managers shall be prepared to demonstrate factors considered; §1606.3: consider and balance relevant factors.

- https://edpm.dc.gov/chapter/16/
- https://go.mpdconline.com/GO/GO_120_21.pdf

## pd-0174: PERB Rule 559.1 (6-B DCMR § 559.1)

Document: perb_decision_braddock.pdf; location: PDF page 2, ¶7.

Authority type: regulation; as written: Board Rule 559.1; pincite: 559.1.

Cited for: Decision and order is final upon issuance

Quotes: []

Context: 

Fictional by design: False; 

### First check: VERIFIED_MINOR_DEFECT

PERB Rule 559.1 (6-B DCMR § 559.1) exists and governs finality of Board decisions, but it makes a decision final upon service on the parties, not 'upon issuance' as the scenario decision says.

Excerpt read: 6-B DCMR § 559.1: "A decision and order of the Board is final upon service on the parties either through File & ServeXpress or as provided in § 502.10(a)."

- https://www.dcregs.dc.gov/Common/DCMR/RuleList.aspx?ChapterNum=6-B5

### Second check: VERIFIED_MINOR_DEFECT

The PERB-published Rule 559.1 primary text makes a decision and order final upon service on the parties. The fictional PERB order cites that rule but states finality upon issuance. This is a real wording mismatch, although the order may specify an operative finality date under other Board authority; the scenario gives no service date. The Board website also retains an older rule page with different wording, so cite the published rules PDF for this conclusion.

Excerpt read: Rule 559.1: A decision and order of the Board is final upon service on the parties either through File & ServeXpress or as provided in § 502.10(a).

- https://perb.dc.gov/sites/default/files/dc/sites/perb/page_content/attachments/PERB-Rules.pdf

## pd-0600: W.R. Grace & Co. v. Rubber Workers

Document: paperworkers_v_misco.pdf; location: PDF page 1, syllabus.

Authority type: case; as written: W. R. Grace & Co. v. Rubber Workers, 461 U. S. 757, 461 U. S. 766; pincite: 766.

Cited for: Public policy must be explicit, well defined and dominant

Quotes: ['some explicit public policy', 'well defined and dominant, and is to be ascertained by reference to the laws and legal precedents, and not from general considerations of supposed public interests.']

Context: 

Fictional by design: False; 

### First check: VERIFIED

461 U.S. at 766 states that a court may not enforce a collective-bargaining agreement contrary to public policy and that the policy must be well defined and dominant, ascertained from laws and legal precedents.
